# Chintan backlog

The running ledger of what has been asked of Chintan and what was decided: the owner's trial notes, the QA pass's findings and the external reviews' items, from 2026-09-04 on, in dated sections. Completed rows stay, so the file is the record of why things are the way they are as much as the queue of what is next. One line per item. Status is one of **fixing** (in a branch now), **next** (agreed, not started), **design** (needs a decision; the proposal is in the note), **answer** (a question, answered in the note), **question** (waiting on the owner), **declined for now** (deliberately not built; the note says why and when to revisit), **dropped** (the owner withdrew it), **done**.

Two conventions added 2026-10-01 (PR9-17), read by `scripts/backlog-view.py`, which generates `docs/backlog-open.md` — the last row per Id, sorted into *Needs the owner*, *Open, unowned*, *Done, gate owed* and *Reverted / superseded (30 days)*; CI fails when the generated file is stale, so run the script after appending rows. (1) A later row that changes an earlier row's truth is appended, never edited in: its Id cell repeats the earlier row's Id cell exactly (a composite such as `PR-2, PR-4` included; an Id followed by a parenthetical, `R6-RT-8 (reverted)` or `DB6-22 (follow-up)`, counts as that Id), its Item starts with `Correction:`, `Review of #N:`, `Superseded by <id>:` or `Reverted:`, and the generator takes the last row per Id. (2) The Status cell may carry `gate: <what>` on a done row that still owes a check (listed under *Done, gate owed*; `owed`, `**Owner**:`, `device check`, `left to the owner` or `without the live eval` in the Notes counts too; a Correction row replaces the earlier row entirely, so it must restate any gate it still owes) and `owner: <YYYY-MM-DD>` for the date the owner was first asked (otherwise the date in the nearest `##` heading is used, and the status words `owner`, `asked`, `gated` or `question` put the row under *Needs the owner*). Regenerate the view after appending rows and after a rebase; a conflict in `docs/backlog-open.md` is resolved by regenerating it, never keep-both.

## Bugs

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | Transcript panel says "made before timestamps were captured" for every recording; Copy transcript copies nothing | **done** | Root cause found: `segments.json` uses `start_ms`/`end_ms`, the parser expected `start`/`end` in seconds, so every segment was dropped. Backend was fine (`has_segments: true`, file present). Copy also gets a touch-safe fallback and clearer scope ("Copy this transcript" = that recording). |
| B2 | Note shows old text after a recording is filed into it; needs a second open or a reload | **done** | No cache invalidation when a capture reaches `appended`. The filing poll now invalidates the note, the list and the offline corpus on that transition, and on "Open the note". |
| B3 | Filing row should disappear once "Open the note" or Dismiss is tapped, and must not come back | **done** | Dismissed ids move from an in-memory set to `localStorage`; the 10-minute auto-hide is removed (rows stay until you act). |
| B4 | Previous recording's waveform flashes when a new recording starts | **done** | Two causes. First fix: the recorder kept the old session until `getUserMedia` resolved. What remained (N6): the canvas draws its first frame from the child's effect, before the screen's own effect has armed the next recording, so it read the old collector — and its draw effect does not re-run until the stream is live, so that frame stayed on the element for the whole microphone request. |
| B5 | Search does not find words inside transcripts | **done** | Both server and local search only saw title/aliases/tags/snippet. The note index gains `search_text` (lowercased body, ≤32 KB), searched on the server and shipped to the offline corpus; one-time backfill for existing notes. |
| B6 | Version string shows `v0.5.0-14-gabcdef` | **done** | Two parts: deploys now tag `main` (`vX.Y.Z+1`) and publish a release; the footnote shows the tag, or `v0.5.0+14 (abcdef)` when between tags. |
| B7 | Passkey setup is never offered | **done** (hand-off) | Confirmed in a browser: Cognito's managed login does not prompt for passkey registration after a password sign-in; it only offers "sign in with passkey" to users who already have one. In-app registration was tried against prod (2026-09-04) and is impossible: `StartWebAuthnRegistration` returns `rp.id` = the managed-login domain, and `navigator.credentials.create` from `vppillai.github.io` fails with `SecurityError: The relying party ID is not a registrable domain suffix of, nor equal to the current domain`; the hosted-UI access token also lacks `aws.cognito.signin.user.admin`, so even listing credentials is refused. What works is the managed login's own page, `/passkeys/add?client_id=…&redirect_uri=…`, which needs a live managed-login session and returns to the app with `?result=success` or `?result=invalid_session`. Shipped: a "Sign in with a passkey" card on **You** that hands off to that page and reports the result, a one-time nudge on the library (dismissal remembered per device), and unsupported-browser copy. Listing/removing passkeys in-app would need the client's OAuth scopes to include `aws.cognito.signin.user.admin` (infra). |
| B8 | Upload feels slow even for tiny recordings | **done** | Measured in the 2026-09-04 log review (§3): the server's `POST /v1/captures` is 21 ms p50 and never cold; the client's audio PUT is 1–2 s on a good network and uncorrelated with size; the pipeline takes 2–9 s; and the 4-second poll added 0–4 s before the row read Filed. What shipped (#28): the capture poll runs every 1.5 s for a pending capture's first 30 s and 4 s after (`CAPTURE_POLL_FAST_MS`), and uploads are bounded. The remaining seconds are the providers' (transcribe, route, cleanup). |
| B9 | Filing sometimes takes ~1 minute | **done** | One routing call to MiniMax took 63 s; others take 1–2 s. The log review checks whether this is provider variance or our prompt/settings and proposes a timeout + fallback. |
| B10 | "Add a recording to this note" → after Stop the filed text isn't in the note until re-entering | **done** | Same cause as B2. |
| B11 | Manual edit: how many API calls? | **answer** | One PATCH per pause in typing (autosave debounces; since yesterday saves are serialised, one in flight at a time, and the version comes from the response). The QA pass counts requests for a typed sentence; if it is more than ~1 per pause, we widen the debounce. |
| B12 | Recording continues when the phone screen locks? | **design** | Android Chrome keeps a `MediaRecorder` alive with the screen locked as long as the tab holds the mic (it shows a notification); iOS Safari suspends it. The app already holds a wake lock, which keeps the screen ON instead. Proposal: keep the wake lock (safer for eyes-off use), and add an explicit "screen may lock" mode later only if Android testing shows recording survives it. Needs a real-device test. |
| B13 | Incoming call while recording | **done** | The track `mute`/`ended` handlers exist; make `mute` auto-pause (not just flag) and show "Paused: microphone taken by another app" live, resume on `unmute`. |
| B14 | PWA shortcut / launch latency | **next** | The "Record a thought" shortcut ships in the manifest (→ `/capture`). Launch latency was measured by QA (cold load of `/` and `/capture` on Fast 3G, 4× CPU) but none of the candidates has landed as of v0.5.9: the bundle is one 534 KB chunk (170 KB gzip), the capture route is not precached and it still fetches notes/tags/archived. Those three remain the work; the preconnect to the API and Cognito has landed (`vite.config.ts` injects both hints and the live index carries them). |
| B15 | A recording made into a note keeps the spoken filing instruction ("Create a note with the title …") in the body, while a routed one has it removed | **done** | Live QA 2026-09-05 §5a. The targeted path skipped routing and with it the removal of instruction spans. It now makes the same span-extraction call as routing when the transcript contains an instruction cue ("add this to", "create a note", "title this", … — `routing.MentionsInstruction`), with the target note as the only candidate, removes the spans and ignores the destination; no cue, no call. Priced as `route` through the breaker; a failed call keeps the words as spoken. |
| B16 | The router created a second note with the title of an existing active note | **done** | Live QA 2026-09-05 §5b. Q10's rule — an existing note with the same title is the destination — was only in the prompt. It now holds in code after the model answers: a `new` decision whose title matches an active candidate's title or alias (case- and whitespace-insensitive) appends to that note with confidence 1 and logs the note id. |
| B17 | An Ask answer saved as a note is cited as a source by later questions | **done** | Live QA 2026-09-05 §4. Notes tagged `ask` — the tag the app puts on a saved thread — are left out of Ask retrieval (`ask.Retrievable`), so an answer never cites an earlier answer. The frontend sets the tag when it saves. |

## Features needing design

| # | Item | Status | Proposal |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Advanced cleanup ("smart") vs the current misheard-word cleanup, with raw and cleaned as tabs; re-run over the whole note when new audio arrives | **done** | Backend in #36, the Cleaned tab (Structured / Polished, "Keep it updated after each recording", Generate) in #37. Per-recording cleanup stays as it is (`faithful`/`polished` per capture). The note gains one **cleaned view** of the entire body — `structured` (Markdown: short headings, lists for enumerations, filler and repetition removed) or `polished` (prose only, light touch) — generated by the worker's `clean-note` task from the body with the markers stripped, one LLM call through the breaker (`op: clean_note`, input capped at 150 KB, output at 200 KB, refused whole rather than truncated), stored on the note row (`cleaned_body`, promoted like `search_text`). `POST /v1/notes/{id}/clean` (optional `{mode}`) answers 202 and invokes the worker; `PATCH` takes `auto_clean` and `cleaned_mode`; `NoteDetail.cleaned` is `null` or `{body, mode, generated_at, stale, error?}`. Every body write — editor save, append, move, delete — marks an existing view **stale**; with `auto_clean` on, an append (the worker invokes itself), a move or a delete (the API invokes the worker) regenerates it; an editor save never does. The view is **read-only**: edits belong in the body, and the view is regenerated from it. A failed run keeps the previous view and stores a fixed `cleaned.error`. Both exports carry the view (`note.cleaned` in the JSON export, `<slug>-<id>.cleaned.md` from `chintanctl export`). `search_text` stays derived from the raw body. |
| D2 | Move a misfiled recording to another note; splice text by timestamp | **done** | Recording rows get **Move to…** (single) and multi-select on recordings. Moving = remove that capture's marker-delimited paragraph from note A (we have exact markers since yesterday), append it to note B at the position implied by the recording's time relative to B's recordings (chronological insert, not always at the end), re-point the capture row. The transcript is not re-run. Structured mode (D1) re-runs on both notes. |
| D3 | Delete a single recording and its text | **done** | Same primitive as D2's removal step: delete the marker-delimited paragraph, delete the capture row and objects (audio, raw, clean, segments, peaks), typed confirmation. |
| D4 | Download all recordings of a note as one archive; multi-select recordings | **done** | Client-side zip (`fflate`, ~8 KB) from the presigned audio URLs; name files `<note>-<yyyymmdd-hhmm>.webm`; no backend change. Selection UI shared with D2/D3. |
| D5 | AI search / ask the knowledge base | **done** | Frontend landed: **Ask** as a mode of the search field (Search · Ask segment, `mode=ask` in the URL, the question never is), the Ask panel with the answer, "Not in your notes" when ungrounded, source chips that open the note, follow-ups sent with the last six turns as `history`, Save as note, Clear; the thread lives in `sessionStorage`. Backend 2026-09-05: `POST /v1/ask` (202, idempotent) + `GET /v1/ask/{askId}`; the worker's `ask` task ranks the notes lexically over title/aliases/tags/`search_text`, packs the top bodies within a 40k-rune budget, one grounded LLM call through the breaker (`ops.ask`), sources filtered to what was packed; `ASK#` rows, 24 h TTL. No per-note summary — the bodies are cheap to read for a dozen notes. Embeddings deliberately not built; see `docs/design/ask.md`. |
| D6 | Per-user usage accounting for future admin/billing | **done** | Monthly and daily `USAGE#<user>#…` counters written by the breaker (cost, calls, audio seconds, tokens) plus `GET /v1/usage`. 2026-09-05: per-provider split, `api_requests` per authenticated request, `storage` summary, the tenant's share of the AWS cost, and the `chintanctl usage` admin listing over GSI1. |
| D7 | Transcription language per note (English default, other language, or auto/mixed) | **done** | Groq Whisper takes an ISO-639-1 `language`, or auto-detects when omitted; there is no explicit mixed mode, and auto is not one: it picks one language per recording and drops or re-scripts the rest (round 3, T3), so mixed speech does best with the main language's code. Per-note `language` (`en` default, any code, or `auto`) + a `default_language` setting. UI surfaced 2026-09-05: first field of the note's **Details** panel (was hidden in Tags), named in the note's meta line (tap opens Details), and above retention on You; 28 languages with native names. |
| D8 | Offline recording; notes readable offline | **answer / next** | Recording offline works: chunks stay in IndexedDB, and since Q11 (#31, `useResendOnReconnect`) the send goes out on reconnect without a tap. Notes are cached for offline reading (the list and the search corpus); a note body is cached after its first open. Prefetching bodies so an unopened note reads offline is the half still to do. |
| D9 | Telemetry for data-driven improvement | **design** | Backend: already emits EMF metrics per stage and provider latency; add client-observed timings (record→sent, sent→filed, cold start) as one small `POST /v1/telemetry` batch per session, written to the log only (no new storage). Frontend: `web-vitals` is *not* bundled (the console errors you pasted come from a browser extension). Keep it log-only until a question needs more. |
| D10 | "Febl index 01" ring integration | **dropped** | Owner dropped it on 2026-09-05. The device and its outbound integration were never identified; had it gone ahead, the path would have been a small `POST /v1/inbox` with a per-device token. |
| D11 | Docs cleanup: remove v1/v2 history; a single-script deploy for a fresh clone | **done** | History documents removed; the 2026-09-03 review is kept as a dated report under `docs/history/`. README rewritten as: what it is · deploy · configure · operate · security · develop. `scripts/doctor.sh` checks the prerequisites and prints the next command. Verified with a fresh checkout and the scripts' dry runs. |
| D12 | Prompt-injection safety for AI features | **next** | D1/D5 add LLM calls over user content; they reuse the fenced-transcript pattern and the router's output verifier. |

## From the QA pass (docs/qa/2026-09-04/report.md)

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Download audio never works (CORS: the `<audio>` element's no-CORS cached response poisons the later `fetch`) | **done** | `crossOrigin="anonymous"` on the element or `cache: 'no-store'` on the fetch. |
| Q2 | Re-open a note within ~30 s of editing shows the pre-edit text; next save 409s with a false "changed elsewhere" | **done** | The PATCH result is never written to the note/list query cache. Write-through on save; same root cause as Q3. |
| Q3 | Library rows stale after editing a note | **done** | See Q2. |
| Q4 | Filed receipts accumulate; dismissal not persisted | **done** (the QA sampler had misread the new `uploading` state; two real defects behind it fixed in #31) | PR #22 was meant to fix this; QA ran on that build and still saw it. Re-test, then fix. |
| Q5 | In-app URLs drop the trailing slash → outside the PWA scope; offline reload of such a URL is a browser error page | **done** | Router `basename` normalisation. Check installed-PWA behaviour on a real device after. |
| Q6 | Bulk-action bar sits below the last row (6,400 px down on a phone) | **done** | Folded into U2 (sticky bar above the tab bar). |
| Q7 | Note opened mid-filing never refreshes | **done** | Same fix family as B2/Q2. |
| Q8 | One `GET /v1/search` per keystroke | **done** | Debounce 250 ms; the local corpus stays instant. |
| Q9 | Unsaved settings silently lost on navigation | **done** | Save on change (each control), no Save button. |
| Q10 | Same speech, six runs, four outcomes, including a duplicate "Roof repair" note | **done** | Router redesign in progress; add "an existing note with the same title is the destination" rule. |
| Q11 | Offline recording waits for a manual Send after reconnect | **done** | Queue the send; see D8. |
| Q12 | Passkey enrolment never offered | **done** | See B7. |
| Q13–Q21 | Theme "Unsaved" flag, no `h1` on note, 20 px checkboxes, orphan chunk row per Cancel, tag chip outlives its notes, offline "Loading…" forever, fixed 12-row textarea, skip-link shadow, `has_peaks` on a peak-less capture (fixed server-side in #23) | **done** | Batch of small fixes. |

Performance (cold, Fast 3G + 4× CPU): library interactive ~2.95 s, `/capture` ~3.05 s; one 136 KB gzip JS chunk; four API calls on the library, one only for the "Archived · n" count.

## Order of work

1. B1–B6, B10 and U6 (frontend and backend branches in progress).
2. QA pass results folded in.
3. B7 passkey registration; U10/U11/U12 (send hand-off, review playback); U2/U4 selection and record button; B13 call handling.
4. D1 cleanup modes (needs your call on the three-mode proposal), D2/D3/D4 recording actions.
5. D5 Ask, D9 telemetry, D11 docs.

Historical: this is the 4 September plan, kept as written; every item on it but D9 (telemetry, still at design) shipped by round 5 (PR9-22, 2026-10-01).

## Added 2026-09-05

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| N5 | Live waveform much smaller than the review player's for the same recording | **done** | The live canvas drew raw RMS while `envelope()` normalised to the running peak. `PeakCollector.recent()` now divides by the same scale, with a floor (`PEAK_FLOOR`) in both so silence stays flat; one `BAR_HEIGHT_SCALE` for the live canvas, the review scrubber and the note's scrubber. |
| N6 | Previous recording's waveform still flashed for the length of the microphone request | **done** | See B4. The store retires the old session before dispatching `request`, `amplitudes()` answers only while the machine has a live recording, and the canvas is keyed by the recording's local id. |
| N7 | Recordings made into a note also appeared as filing rows on Home | **done** | Home shows only non-targeted captures (contract §3): `CaptureWire.targeted`, plus a per-device `localStorage` set of ids the uploader sent with a `noteId` for backends that predate the flag; the local upload row on Home hides when the upload is aimed at a note. The note's Recordings tab is unchanged. |
| N8 | Swipe a note or recording row for its actions on a phone | **done** | `SwipeRow` + `useSwipeActions` (pointer events, trailing tray, snap on the motion tokens, one open at a time, rubber-band, off for a fine pointer and during bulk-select). Notes: Archive · Delete in the library, Restore · Delete in the archive — delete behind the typed-title dialog, archive-then-purge from the library because the server refuses to purge an active note. Recordings: Move · Delete, opening the row menu's own sheet and dialog. |
| N9 | Long press selects text; the app "feels like a website" | **done** | Controls, rows, chips, tabs and the bars give up `user-select`, the iOS callout and the tap flash (base.css), and get a drawn `:active` state; note text, transcripts, the cleaned view and fields stay selectable. `useLongPress` cancels Android's `contextmenu` while a press is armed — now pinned by a test. |
| N10 | The UI is minimalist but a little too flat | **done** | Depth tokens: `--shadow-xs`, refined sm/md/lg, `--shadow-inset`, `--shadow-highlight`, `--gradient-lift`/`--gradient-tonal`, `--color-ground-deep`, `--color-raised-2` (Nocturne's equals raised — the contrast budget is spent there; documented). Cards on shadow-xs, lifting on hover; banner and note tab strip get a hairline and shadow; tab, selection and action bars the sheet shadow; the record button a deeper shadow and a top highlight; chips a tonal fill; filled primaries a top-edge lift; fields recessed; sheets and dialogs on raised-2 with shadow-lg. No layout or spacing change. |
| N11 | Richer per-user usage view | **done** | `GET /v1/usage` grew `providers`, `api.requests`, `storage`, per-day `api_requests` and `aws.share_micros`; You shows the split by provider under Providers, Clean note and Ask among the stages, a facts row (API requests · recordings stored with minutes and MB, approx. when capped · notes), "Your estimated share" under AWS with the Total switching to providers + share when the backend sends one (the label says which), and a dot per day for API requests on the sparkline. Every new field is optional on the frontend so it can deploy first. |
| N12 | Search inside a note | **done** | Find bar under the tab strip (lens in the header, Ctrl/⌘+F on desktop): case- and diacritics-insensitive, "3 of 12", Enter/Shift+Enter, Escape. ## Hardening 2026-09-05 (external review)

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| H1 | Notes list drained only the 500 most recently CREATED notes, then ordered by touch — an old note dictated into today was missing past 500 notes | **done** | The drain reads the whole partition, bounded by a safety ceiling `repository.MaxNotesDrained` (5,000; ≈5 MB of projected rows per list at the ceiling). Hitting it sets `Page.Truncated`; the notes service logs at WARN and counts `NotesListTruncated`. The router's 500-note candidate pool is gone (the store already orders by touch). No index: the v3 decision stands; the index is the remedy if a tenant ever nears the ceiling. |
| H2 | Transcription, cleanup and clean-note had no deadline but the HTTP client's 840 s; a stall held the 900 s Lambda and then failed the capture for good | **done** | `pipeline.Config` `TranscribeTimeout` 5 min, `CleanupTimeout` 2 min, `CleanNoteTimeout` 3 min, applied inside the breaker's `Do`. A deadline is retryable: the capture stays in its stage, Lambda retries, `run` resumes at the missing artefact. `ProviderTimedOut{Stage}`. See `docs/design/pipeline-deadlines.md`. |
| H3 | A model with no price row priced at zero, so the spend cap enforced nothing and nobody knew | **done** | `meter.PriceTable.Resolve`/`Resolves`; the worker refuses to start (`log.Fatalf` naming the key and `meter.DefaultPrices`) when the STT or LLM model resolves to no row; a wildcard-priced model is counted once per process as `PriceWildcardUsed{Provider}`. Runtime fallback unchanged. |
| H4 | `DailySpendCapMicros` defaulted to 0 (no cap) | **done** | Default 5000000 ($5/day); the description names 0 as the deliberate opt-out. Both live instances pass explicit values and are unchanged. `scripts/list-instances.sh` and both instance configs no longer say absent means 0. README row for `daily_spend_cap_micros` still says `0 (count, never enforce)` — README belongs to another stream. |
| H5 | Pin `ci.yaml` like the deploy workflows | **done** | Actions on the SHAs `deploy-*.yaml` already uses, plus `actions/upload-artifact@043fb46d…` (v7.0.1). golangci-lint is fetched from its `v2.13.2` tag and told to install v2.13.2 — the release the unpinned `HEAD` script was resolving anyway, so no new findings; govulncheck `@v1.7.0`. Dependabot's `github-actions` ecosystem already covered every workflow, so the pins get bump PRs. |
| H6 | Document the tenant model | **done** | README → Security → Tenancy: one Cognito user is one tenant, what keys off that (partition, S3 prefixes, `chintanctl`, usage rows, ownership-by-key), and what changes for sharing or multi-user tenants. |
| H7 | Refactor `pipeline.go` into explicit stage objects | **next** | 1,421 lines today, but the stages are already sequential functions that each persist their artefact. Split when the next stage is added, not before: a refactor with no feature behind it is churn the `-race` suite then has to re-earn. |

Idempotent per day by a condition on the month row's `storage_snapshot_day`. | R8b | Gateway-answered CORS preflights (#51) | **reverted** | Verified on staging by a browser check before prod: with `OptionsRoute` gone every preflight answered 401 and all browser requests failed (`net::ERR_FAILED`). The mechanism, now written into the template's `CorsConfiguration` comment (S4): HTTP API's `CorsConfiguration` auto-answers only a preflight that matches NO route, and `$default` matches OPTIONS and carries the JWT authorizer. The explicit OPTIONS route stays — the Lambda answers 204 and the gateway rewrites the CORS headers from `CorsConfiguration` on the way out, which is why the live 204 carries the gateway's seven header names. Every preflight is therefore one Lambda invocation, by decision. Prod gate rejected automatically; reverted in #54. |
| S4 | CORS comments contradicted each other and the mechanism that broke staging (`CorsConfiguration`, `OptionsRoute`, R8b, `middleware/cors.go`) | **done** | All four now say the one verified thing (see R8b): the gateway auto-answers only an unmatched OPTIONS; `$default` matches it and its authorizer answers 401; `OptionsRoute` sends it to the Lambda, whose 204 the gateway rewrites from `CorsConfiguration`. `cors.go`'s comment says the gateway's list wins and that its own list is for runs without the gateway. Not done here: the middleware's constant still lacks `X-Correlation-Id` and no test compares the two lists — a backend follow-up if a local run without the gateway ever matters. |

Try again on a turn that never reached this side resends it the same way; a turn the server itself answered is still asked afresh. `useAskThread.test.tsx` covers remount-before-202, the in-flight replay and retry-after-a-lost-POST. |

| S1 | An editor save landing between the worker's body write and its index refresh silently deleted the paragraph just dictated: the version was read before the refresh bumped it and the ETag after the write, so both checks passed and the marker was carried without its text (review 2026-09-05 r2, D-H1) | **done** | Three parts. (1) `Pipeline.append` stamps the note row before it writes the body — one conditional `UpdateItem` (`Store.StampNoteAppend`: `version+1`, `appending_capture`, `appending_at`), never a whole-row `PutNote` — and `refreshNoteIndex` clears the stamp with its `PutNote`; an append that hands its claim back clears it with `Store.ClearNoteAppend`. Two appends to one note wait on each other's stamp (`appendStampWait`) so the row's one stamp always covers the write in flight. (2) `UpdateNote` refuses a body write with `ErrAppendInProgress` while the stamp is younger than `AppendClaimLease`, checked before the version so the client is not sent round the F14 rebase into the window; the body's ETag is read before the row so the version is the later witness. (3) The wire: `PATCH /v1/notes/{id}` → 409 in the version-conflict shape plus `reason: append_in_progress` and `Retry-After: 2`; the client repeats the same save unchanged. Tests: `pipeline/append_autosave_race_test.go` (the interleaving, the same-text re-save, the re-read on a moved version, the release), `service/notes_test.go` (read order, stamp before version), the contract fixture `problemAppendInProgress`. `docs/design/append-vs-autosave.md`. |
| S2 | Clean-note supersession was judged from the row's stamp, not the invocation's: with two requests in flight (structured then polished) and the newer run landing first, the older run read an unstamped row, called the model and stored structured over the polished view asked for last; a Lambda redelivery of a finished run billed a second call the same way (review 2026-09-05 r2, C-B1; reopens R5 narrowly) | **done** | `Invocation.RequestedAt` carries the stamp `RecordCleanRequest` wrote, through `Invoker.InvokeCleanNote(…, requestedAt)` on every hand-off (`RequestClean`, both auto-clean paths, the worker's self-invoke). `CleanNote` judges against it: the row must still carry exactly that stamp and mode, and an unstamped row means a newer run has already answered. An empty payload stamp (queued before the field) is judged from the row as before. Tests: `TestAnOlderCleanRunDeliveredAfterTheNewerOneWritesNothing`, `TestARedeliveredCleanRunThatAlreadyWroteDoesNotCallTheModelAgain`, the payload round trip, the hand-off carrying the stamp. |
| S9 | The clean-request stamp rewrote the whole note row (≤200 KB with `cleaned_body`, from whatever copy the caller held) and bumped `version`, which is what forced the F14 client-side rebase (review 2026-09-05 r2, C-B6) | **done** | `Store.StampCleanRequest` / `ClearCleanStamp`: one conditional `UpdateItem` on `cleaned_requested_at`/`_mode` under the row's version, version unchanged, no other attribute touched; `RecordCleanRequest` and `ClearCleanRequest` use them. The wire list keeps its pagination for the client corpus, but a page that asks for `search_text`/`cleaned_body` drains light and fetches the large field for its 200 rows with `BatchGetItem` (`hydrateNotes`, unprocessed keys re-asked). OpenAPI documents the outcome. Tests: `TestPurgeNotesListsTheUnindexedCapturesOncePerBatch`, `TestPurgeNotesStopsAtItsTimeBudgetAndReportsTheRestForRetry`, `TestPurgeNotesHonoursANearerRequestDeadline`. | S11 | The UI offered Retry 10 minutes in (`STUCK_AFTER_MS`) while the append lease is 20 (`AppendClaimLease`); every tap in minutes 10–20 on an `appending` capture dead-lettered and e-mailed (review 2026-09-05 r2, D-L5) | **done** (backend) | The honest side is the API's: `RetryCapture` refuses an `appending` capture with 409 `ErrCaptureInFlight` ("the capture is still being processed; wait for it to finish or fail") while the claim lease is live, so a tap inside it is a message, not a dead letter. Documented on `POST /v1/captures/{id}/retry`. The client's threshold (20 min for `appending`, 15 for the other in-flight stages) is the frontend stream's; until then the early tap shows the 409's detail (S14). |
| S12 | Retry on a capture still `transcribing`/`cleaning` started a second concurrent delivery (review 2026-09-05 r2, D-L9) | **done** | `CaptureIndex.LastProgressAt` is stamped by every pipeline write (`persist`) and every hand-off (`RetryCapture`, `SetCaptureTarget`); `RetryCapture` refuses an in-flight capture with 409 `ErrCaptureInFlight` until the row has gone `service.CaptureStuckAfter` (15 min, the worker function's timeout) without a write — after which no worker can be alive on it — and then hands it over once; two taps are one delivery. Rows from before the field read `created_at`. `TestRetryCaptureRefusesAnInFlightCaptureUntilNoWorkerCanBeAlive`. |
| S13 | Three definitions of "terminal": `chintanctl reconcile` reported a `spend_capped` capture as `stuck_capture` (review 2026-09-05 r2, D-L3) | **done** | `model.IsTerminalStatus` (appended, no_content, failed, spend_capped, needs_target) is the one definition; `service.CaptureIsTerminal` delegates to it, `RetryCapture` and reconcile read it, and the frontend's `TERMINAL_CAPTURE_STATUSES` lists the same five. `TestReconcileDoesNotReportSpendCappedOrNeedsTargetAsStuck`. |
| S15 | `share_micros` could exceed `month_micros` two to three months back: the tenant month row never expires while the instance `SPEND#<day>` rows carry a 90-day TTL, so the denominator shrank under the numerator (review 2026-09-05 r2, D-L6) | **done** | `shareOf` clamps the fraction to one: a tenant total at or above the instance total is the whole month's AWS cost. `TestShareOfIsClampedToTheWholeMonth`. |
| S17 | Every captures list made a sequential `NoteExists` GetItem per distinct filed note, on the 1.5 s poll (review 2026-09-05 r2, C-B3) | **done** | `Store.NotesExist(ids)` — one `BatchGetItem` projected to the key — and the list asks it once per page for the notes not yet known (`noteExistence.learn`). `TestListCapturesAsksAboutAPagesNotesInOneRoundTrip`, `TestNotesExistIsOneBatchGetProjectedToTheKey`. |
| S18 | The "no user content in logs" CI check scanned only `backend/internal/provider` (review 2026-09-05 r2, C-B4) | **done** | `scripts/check-log-hygiene.sh` defaults to `provider`, `pipeline` and `service`. It flagged one line — the transcribed-capture log's `obs.Redact(result.Text)`, which was already shape-only; the shape is now taken on its own line so the emitter names no content. |
| S19 | `cleanRequestInFlight`'s 3-minute window is the model call's bound, not the run's; a run over 3 min lets a same-mode repeat start a second, duplicate-billed run (review 2026-09-05 r2, C-B5) | **done** (documented) | Kept at 3 minutes and documented on `CleanNoteTimeout`: with S2 the second run's re-stamp supersedes the first, so the view is never written twice and the cost of the rare slow run is one extra model call; the alternative — a window as long as the worker's 900 s lifetime — would leave a dead run's stamp answering "queued" for fifteen minutes with nothing running. |
| S25 | `Tokenize` kept the first 32 distinct terms in spoken order, so a long question whose subject came last ranked on the preamble (review 2026-09-05 r2, C-B7) | **done** | Over `MaxQueryTerms` the longest terms are kept (earliest spoken among equals), returned in spoken order. `TestTokenizeKeepsAtMostMaxQueryTermsAndPrefersTheLongest`. |
| S29 | A capture whose destination note was purged before the worker reached it still dead-lettered (round-1 B-L8, reopened) | **done** | `run()` on `ErrNotFound` for the destination clears `note_id` and `target_source`, sets `needs_target`, counts `CaptureDestinationPurged` and returns nil; the transcript and cleaned text are kept and the person's answer resumes from them. `TestACaptureWhoseDestinationWasPurgedAsksForANewNoteInsteadOfDeadLettering`. |
| S7 | One execution role for both Lambdas gave the internet-facing API the worker's grants: both provider keys via SSM, `dynamodb:Scan` across tenants, `sqs:SendMessage`, `budgets:ViewBudget` (review 2026-09-05 r2, D-M4) | **done** | `ApiFunctionRole` (`chintan-lambda-api-<i>-<e>`): item ops + Query + BatchGetItem on the table and its indexes, object CRUD and tagging under the bucket, `lambda:InvokeFunction` on the worker's live alias. `WorkerFunctionRole` (`chintan-lambda-worker-<i>-<e>`): the same plus Scan, SSM `GetParameter` on the two keys, self-invoke, SQS on the DLQ; `WorkerReadsBudgetPolicy` attaches to it alone; `WorkerLambdaEventInvokeConfig` depends on it. Both names keep the `chintan-lambda-` prefix the agent boundary and deny policies gate SSM/KMS on, and sit inside bootstrap.yaml's `chintan-*` CreateRole scope with the boundary attached. `dynamodb:BatchGetItem` is granted to both — S5 and S17 introduced the call. Deploy replaces the single role with two; the functions are updated in place. |
| S34 | `template.yaml` S3 notification suffix `/audio.bin` that no code path mints; worker `USER_POOL_ID`/`USER_POOL_CLIENT_ID` read by nothing in `cmd/worker` (review 2026-09-05 r2, D-L10) | **done** | Both removed. `bin` is only the download filename's fallback (`recordingFilename`); every accepted content type maps to webm/m4a/mp3/ogg/wav, each of which keeps its rule. |

## Capture screen 2026-09-21

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| CS1 | Recording could only be stopped, then sent from review: two taps, and a screen to read, for the one thing the app is for | **done** | Send sits in the recording row. `store.stopAndSend(api)` sets a request the store's `dispatch` consumes when the machine leaves `stopping` — `review` sends, anything else drops it — because MediaRecorder hands over its last chunk after `stop()` returns, so `await stop(); await send()` would read a buffer still being written. The screen leaves to `captureReturnPath(noteId, true)` at once, as Send from review does; a failed upload lands on the filing row as before. Stop → review is unchanged. Tests: `store.test.ts` (same create body as Stop→Send under the real late-chunk timing; nothing sent when the stop finds no audio), `CaptureScreen.test.tsx`, `e2e/capture.spec.ts` (a real recorder: the create carries a length and a size). |
| CS2 | The controls were text pills; the owner asked for graphic controls that read at a glance while walking | **done** | Round icon discs with a word under each (the word is the accessible name, so every existing `getByRole('button', { name })` still holds): Cancel ×, Pause ‖ / Resume ▶, Stop ■, Send ↑ while recording; Discard, Re-record ↺, Send at review; Discard/Close and Try again on failure; Done after an upload. Send is the row's only filled shape (ink, like the app's other primaries; the accent stays the record button's). 64 px discs under a coarse pointer, 56 px under a fine one (`--capture-control-size`); the row is four equal `minmax(0, 1fr)` grid columns, so it cannot wrap at 320 px — pinned by a Pixel-7-at-320 e2e. `Icon.tsx` gains `pause` and `send` in the 1.75 px set; Re-record wears the existing `restore` arrow. |
| CS3 | The capture screen's styles sat in three places in `shell.css` | **done** | Moved unchanged into `styles/capture.css` (imported after `shell.css`) in its own commit, then restyled there. The live canvas (`.waveform`), the review player and the target chooser stay in `shell.css`; the shared filled-primary rule there now names `.capture__control--primary .capture__control-disc`. |
| CS4 | The PWA shortcut (`/capture` starts recording at once) | **open** | Unchanged on purpose; the owner's remark is queued as a question. |

The gesture was nulled one frame after it committed, `--swipe-x` stayed `0px` on Home, the archive and recording heads, and the lift's click opened the note instead (since e1e11d5, #53; bisected b055156 → 924f0a0). Cognito then refuses the grant and every token it minted; the id token already in a copier's hands passes the API's stateless check until it expires (≤1 h), and nothing shorter exists without a denylist. | Q1 | A Details change (language, kind, word-for-word, auto-clean) rides the whole draft, so the PATCH carried an unchanged `body`; `CarryCaptureMarkers` treated any body as an edit and moved every marker to the end, detaching each recording from its paragraph — "Transcribe again" then appended a second copy and "Delete recording" left the words behind (QA final, finding 1) | **done** (backend) | `CarryCaptureMarkers` returns the stored body byte for byte when the edited text reads the same with markers stripped; otherwise each marker is put back ahead of its paragraph where that paragraph is still in the edited body verbatim (aligned by exact paragraph text on its own lines, one occurrence per marker) and carried at the end only when the paragraph was rewritten or deleted. `TestCarryCaptureMarkersKeepsAMarkerBesideItsUnchangedParagraph` (unchanged, one edited, deleted, moved, same sentence twice), `TestRetranscribingAfterAnUnchangedBodySaveReplacesInPlace`, `TestDeleteCaptureAfterAnUnchangedBodySaveCutsTheParagraph`. The frontend half — send `body` only when it changed — is QA-frontend's. |
| Q2 | Under a chosen language "Transcribe again" looked like it did nothing, and `capture.language` was null on the wire after an explicit request (QA final, finding 2) | **done** (backend + OpenAPI; the chip is frontend's) | Reproduced on prod with `~/r3/lang/{en,ml}.webm`: the transcription IS re-run in the requested language — `{"language":"hi"}` on the English clip came back in Devanagari, `ml` on the Malayalam clip changed the text, `en` changed it back, and segments.json's `language` follows the request — so the byte-identical run QA saw was Whisper transcribing English speech verbatim under a forced `ml`, as `model/types.go` documents. What was wrong: the `Capture` wire schema carried no language at all, so "remembered on the capture" was unobservable. `Capture.language` (`auto` or the code the transcript was asked for, a `/retranscribe` choice outranking the note's) and `Capture.language_detected` (the provider's name for what it heard) are on the wire, in OpenAPI and `CaptureWire`; fixtures regenerated. Under a chosen language the provider echoes it back, so "Heard as X" can only differ under `auto`. |
| Q4 | Ask answers cited raw `note_<id>` ids in prose, which the renderer italicised (QA final, finding 4) | **done** | The system prompt says to refer to a note by its title in the answer and keep ids to `sources`; `ask.NameNotesInProse` replaces any `note_[0-9a-f_]+` left in the answer with the packed note's title (an unknown one is removed) before the answer is stored. `TestNameNotesInProseSwapsIdsForTitles`, `TestAskTaskNamesNotesByTitleInTheStoredAnswer`. |
| Q1-r4 | The in-place alignment from Q1 re-attached a marker to its paragraph without looking at what followed, but a paragraph runs to the next marker or the end of the body — so text typed below a recording (or between two) joined its paragraph, and Delete cut it, Move carried it, Transcribe again replaced it (review 2026-09-21 r4, High). Also: a Details save with the same body still rewrote it and marked the cleaned view stale; `NameNotesInProse` doubled a title the model had bracketed after the id and left a double space for an unknown one (Lows) | **done** (backend) | `CarryCaptureMarkers` keeps a marker beside its paragraph only when the paragraph opens the body or follows a blank line and nothing but line breaks stands between its end and the next kept marker or the end of the body; walked from the end, since a demoted marker hands what follows it to the marker before — so typing below the last of two recordings carries both markers, which is the honest answer while the rule says where a paragraph starts but not where it ends (an end fence would fix that; not done here). `UpdateNote` skips the body write, snippet and stale marking when the carried body equals the stored bytes. `NameNotesInProse` matches `note_<hex>_<hex>` with an optional bracketed title (emitted once), calls an unknown id "a note", and catches an italicised `_note_…_`. Tests: eight new carry cases, `TestDeleteCaptureAfterTypingBelowTheParagraphKeepsTheTypedText`, `TestADetailsSaveWithTheSameBodyDoesNotMarkTheViewStale`, five new naming cases. |
| Q1-fe | Changing a note's language (or kind, verbatim, auto-clean) in Details sent the whole draft, body included; the server took the unchanged body as an edit, carried every recording's marker to the end, and "Transcribe again" then duplicated the paragraph while Delete recording cut nothing (QA final 2026-09-21, finding 1) | **done** (frontend half; `CarryCaptureMarkers` keeping markers in place for an unchanged body is the backend stream's) | `patchFor` sends `version` plus only the fields that differ from `EditorModel.server`, the draft the server holds at that version: a Details-only change PATCHes without `body`. `server` stays put across an offline save so the queued PATCH (which replaces the one before it) stays cumulative, and becomes theirs on Take theirs / Keep mine / Keep both so Keep mine still writes back everything of mine that differs; `rebase` carries a flushed queue's text onto the new version and leaves the cleaned-view settings alone. append_in_progress and conflict paths unchanged. `autosave.test.ts` (six cases), `useNoteEditor.test.tsx`. |
| Q4-fe | Ask answers cited raw note ids (`note_18d2…_ff6e… (Stale check mobile): "…"`) and the Markdown renderer italicised the `_…_` run (finding 4) | **done** (frontend half; the prompt/corpus shape is the backend stream's) | `thread.nameSources` replaces every `note_[0-9a-f_]{8,}` with the cited source's title, or with "a note" when nothing names it (a dropped id left `: "quote"` orphaned), keeping a name the model put in brackets once; applied in `applyRow` so the thread, its history and Save as note all read the title. `thread.test.ts`. |
| Q6 | The archive view on a phone had no visible label: h1 "Notes · 0", the pressed "Archived" chip off-screen at the far end of the row (finding 6) | **done** | The h1 reads "Archived · N" in the archive; the chip row scrolls sideways to its pressed chip (its own `scrollLeft`, via `chipScrollBy`) until the chip sits a gutter in from the edge — keyed on the row's contents (view, tag, kind, tag names, counts), since on a cold `?view=archived` the tag chips land late from IndexedDB and pushed the chip back off the screen — not `scrollIntoView`, which in Chromium moves the sequential focus navigation starting point and sent the first Tab past the skip link. `NotesScreen.test.tsx`; `archive.spec` reloads the archive on a phone with tags seeded and asserts the chip is in the viewport. |
| Q7 | The Details drawer was a content-height sheet: 715 of 915 px on a phone, over the header on a desktop (finding 7) | **done** | `.note-panel` is capped at `60dvh` with `overflow-y: auto` and `overscroll-behavior: contain` (`notes.css`); at 60 % it also stays under the header on any real desktop. `.note-panel__head` is `position: sticky` at the sheet's top, so Close stays in reach while the content scrolls. |

| Q12 | About's "How filing works" still said "Record into this", the control T6 removed (finding 12) | **done** | "open it and tap the microphone, which then reads *Into this note*, or pick the note on the recording screen". `AboutScreen.test.tsx`. |
| Q13 | The Archive row at the list's end read "Archive · 0" (finding 13) | **done** | The count is hidden at zero, as the chip's is. `NotesScreen.test.tsx`. |
| Q14 | Bulk delete with one note selected did not name it (finding 14) | **done** | "“<title>” and its recordings and transcripts are destroyed…", the row dialog's sentence. `NotesScreen.test.tsx`. |
| Q15 | Offline Home's caption sat on the "TODAY" heading with no gap (finding 15) | **done** | `.library > .screen__count { margin-block-end: var(--space-3) }` in `home.css`, for the offline, loading and result-count captions. |
| Q5 | The offline body prefetch (T44, #72) was a coin flip: five cold loads of Home fetched 20, 1, 1, 20 and 1 bodies, and a first-page note then answered "Not on this device" offline (QA final 2026-09-21, finding 5) | **done** | Home fires four list GETs at mount — active, archived, `kind=checklist`, the search corpus — and each write re-triggers `useCachedNotes`. When the one-row checklist page landed first the pass started on that row alone, and every re-trigger that arrived during its GET found `prefetching` set and was dropped, with nothing to start it again. A trigger during a pass now asks for one more pass (`rowsArrivedMeanwhile`), run when the current one ends; a pass that stopped early for offline or sign-out does not loop, and `attempted` keeps the extra pass to rows no pass has asked for. Vitest reproduces the race with fake IndexedDB (the first page landing while the first body is in the air; fails 1 vs 20 on the old code); `e2e/offline.spec.ts` forces the ordering in Chromium (the checklist page answers at once, the rest are slowed) and was shown to fail 3/3 against the old hook and pass 5/5 against the fix. Live-site check was not possible: the QA pass signed out and revoked the throwaway refresh token (`/oauth2/token` → 400). |

| MOVE-NEW | Move a recording to a note that does not exist yet ("New note…" in the Move sheet); the text must travel with the recording (owner, 2026-09-24) | **done** (backend + OpenAPI; the sheet entry is S4's) | `CaptureMove` takes `note_id` or `new_note_title` (oneOf, the same field, trim and 1–200 runes, and the same sentences as `CaptureTarget`: `supply either note_id or new_note_title`, `… not both`, `title is required`, `title is N characters; the limit is 200`). `MoveCaptureToNewNote` checks the capture first, creates the note through the notes service as `/target` does, then runs the existing move, so the paragraph is cut from the source and inserted as the new note's first — the text was already travelling with the recording on every move; nothing about that changed. A failed move is compensated (`discardUnusedNote`): the note has a fresh id per call and a 5xx is not replayed under its Idempotency-Key, so each retry would have left one more empty note behind "nothing changed"; a new note whose body is still empty (every `ErrMoveIncomplete`) is removed through `NoteCreator.DiscardNote` — body, meta, row, no capture cascade, since a re-point that reported a fault may have landed — and one the paragraph reached is kept and logged by id at Error. Answer: the 200 `Capture` whose `note_id` is the new note, as `/target` answers. ## Owner feedback 2026-09-26

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| OF-RING | The Pebble Index 01 ring's webhook got 401 in 0 ms from `POST /v1/inbox/audio` (2026-09-26 14:21Z): the ring's header setting is a bare key/value, so the key arrived without `Bearer `, and its body is a `multipart/form-data` form (`audio` as `audio/mp4`, `transcription`, `recordedAt`, `client`), which the raw route would have filed as a recording of type `multipart/form-data` | **done** (backend + OpenAPI + `docs/design/inbox.md`; the Devices-card recipe and README lines are the frontend PR) | `deviceKeyOf` (`inbox.go`) takes the one credential in three spellings — `Authorization: Bearer ck_…`, bare `Authorization: ck_…`, `X-Device-Key: ck_…` (read first) — with the same check and the same fixed 401; the threat model is unchanged. `inboxAudioForm`: the form is read with `r.MultipartReader()` under the raw body's 4 MiB cap, envelope included; the `audio` part (its own type; none is the raw path's 400, since RFC 7578 makes an untyped part `text/plain`) is ingested as a raw body is, a `transcription` part alone takes the text route (`acceptInboxText`, `has_audio:false`), both together files the audio and drops the ring's text (the pipeline transcribes with the note's language); `recordedAt` is ignored because a capture has no recorded-at field, `client` names nothing acted on. New fixed 400 `the form has no audio or transcription part`. `inbox_test.go`: the three spellings, the four form shapes, empty form, not-audio part, oversize (413); the conformance 400 scenario walks all four roads. Adversarial review (2026-09-26): retry semantics documented (no `Idempotency-Key` → a second capture; with one, the raw body's fingerprint means a re-encoded form is 409), `X-Device-Key` scoped to non-browser clients (not in the CORS allow-list), a `transcription` part's invalid UTF-8 becomes U+FFFD as on the JSON route, and `X-Chintan-Language`/`-Duration-Ms` are declared ignored on a transcription-only form. |
| OF-DEL | Owner 2026-09-26: "when I delete, I have to type delete. I don't like that UX" — every permanent delete was gated by typing the word | **done** (frontend) | No typed word anywhere. Delete on Home (row ⋮, swipe tray, note header ⋮, selection bar) is the archive on the tap, no dialog, with a shell toast "Deleted · kept in Archive for 30 days" and an Undo button for six seconds (`components/Toast.tsx`, a row above the tab bar like the update prompt; polite live region, real button, so keyboard-reachable); the separate Archive item is gone from Home. In the archive, Delete forever (row, header, bar) is a plain `ConfirmDialog` — the same sentence, one destructive button, focus on Cancel, no field — and a bulk Delete forever of more than ten notes is "Hold to delete N notes", a one-second press under a finger or a mouse (`holdMs`; Enter, Space and an assistive technology's click confirm at once — a held keystroke is a WCAG 2.1.1 timing; fill animation in `--color-destructive`, visible in both themes, whole at once under reduced motion). Undo re-pins a note that was pinned (`useUndoDelete`), since the server's restore comes back unpinned; the toast's timer pauses under a pointer or focus, and its Undo ring is ground on the ink card. Recording deletes are a plain confirm. `requireText`/`requireLabel` and the `useBulkDeleteNotes` archive-then-purge hook are removed as dead code. About's data paragraph and `docs/design/home.md` (new "Deleting" section) describe the flow. Tests: `ConfirmDialog.test.tsx` (plain, hold, early release, keyboard hold), `Toast.test.tsx`, `NoteRow.test.tsx`, `NotesScreen.test.tsx` (Undo returns the row; > 10 needs the hold), `Recordings.test.tsx`; e2e `archive.spec.ts` (Undo by keyboard), `recordings.spec.ts`, `pins.spec.ts`, `swipe.spec.ts`. |

Fix: one extraction call replaces the transcript cleanup for a non-verbatim checklist (`Pipeline.extractItems`, `cleanup.ItemsPrompt`/`ParseItems`, `provider.LLM.Items`) over the **raw** transcript with the list title — `{"items":[…]}`, noun phrases, app words dropped, "X and Y" split, `{"items":[]}` → `no_content` (instruction-only speech adds nothing), title-equal item dropped, >100 items or no list → the recording as one item + `ChecklistItemsDiscarded`. The router's spans and the targeted strip are not applied to checklist content (no router call on the targeted path). `checklistItems` renders N lines under the one marker; `keepTick` carries ticks line for line. Tasks mode: `NoteOutput(mode, raw, body)` refuses an answer whose `- [x]` lines are not the body's, drops an open item whose words are not in the body (`TasksItemsDropped`), and the prompt says a noun-phrase item stays as written. Live eval harness `provider.TestLiveChecklistItems` (skipped unless `LIVE_LLM=1`). `docs/design/checklists.md` rewritten for the append rule and tasks mode. Not built: applying "remove X"/"make that two" to the list (returned as one item as spoken; future `{"add","remove","done"}`), dedupe on append, router span guard for plain notes, `X-Chintan-Note-Id` on `/v1/inbox/text`. |

`GetWithETag` hands out the exact quoted tag and the tag moves with the body; `PutIfMatch` table (stale → `ErrPreconditionFailed` and the body untouched; `""` creates when absent, refuses when present; a tag for a deleted object refused; the current tag writes); `PutTagged` binds `upload.CaptureAudioTags`, plain `Put` binds nothing; `MarkProcessed` twice merges the processed tag beside the retention tags, missing key is nil; `Exists` both ways; Delete of a missing key is nil (S3's 204 — `memory.Objects` says `ErrNotFound` here; every caller tolerates both); every call wraps a transport fault without reading it as a sentinel and refuses a cancelled context; presigned PUT/GET URLs name the bucket, the key, `X-Amz-Expires=900` and a signature, signed offline by a real client with static credentials. `dynamo_test.go`: `UpdateCaptureStatus` (status + error under a version bump, an earlier error cleared, missing → `ErrNotFound`, a writer slipping in between the read and the write → `ErrVersionConflict` with its row kept), `AbandonIdempotent` (a claim released so the retry proceeds; a completed record kept and replayed; nothing claimed is nothing to release), `PutAsk`/`GetAsk` (every field round-trips, the `ttl` attribute is `ExpiresAt`, missing and another tenant's → `ErrNotFound`, no id refused). Found on the way: `fakeDynamo.av` rendered a BOOL as `""`, so `idem_done = :notdone` compared `""` with `""` and held for a completed record — the fake would have deleted what real DynamoDB refuses; it now renders `true`/`false`. `go test -race -cover ./internal/repository/`: 82.6 %, every `s3.go` function covered. `append-vs-autosave.md` names the adapter test as the proof of the ETag contract. |

The owner approved every recommendation in `docs/reviews/2026-09-26/round-5-proposals.md` §2 ("go with your recommendations on all of them"). The prompts stream's PR B shipped them without the live-eval baseline, which the agent cannot run (the key is the owner's); the owner approved measuring on production instead — `backend/internal/provider/testdata/eval/prod-battery.md` (routing) and `orb:~/r3/live/checklist-battery.py` (items) after the merge.

| F2 | Owner: "we need a mechanism to regenerate everything: we are making changes to the prompts and updates to the backend; for notes already created these updates will not be applicable. Even in future when we release new updates, existing notes should be able to take advantage by being able to regenerate the entire thing." | **done** (backend + OpenAPI + chintanctl + frontend; `docs/design/regenerate.md`) | `POST /v1/notes/{id}/regenerate` (202 `{status, captures}`; 409 while a regeneration or an append is in flight; spend cap first) resets every appended recording that has a transcript — a plain note's only while its paragraph is still under its marker, a verbatim note's none — to `transcribed` with its clean artefact and append claim cleared, exactly as Transcribe again does for one, and hands the worker `regenerate-note` with the ids. The worker re-cleans (plain) or re-extracts (checklist) each from the stored transcript — the routed one when there is one — and re-appends by marker, one at a time, then regenerates the whole-note view once if the note has one; nothing is transcribed, the title is untouched, `CaptureRegenerated{Outcome}` counts each and the calls land under the usual `cleanup` op. Progress is the captures' own statuses: the note's poll, the filing strip and the ⋮ item ("Regenerating…") follow them; no note-level flag. A used checklist (every Items-tab save carries the markers) is handled by finding each recording's previous items by their words from its clean artefact, read before the extraction overwrites it (`replaceChecklistItems`); `keepTick` now follows the words, then the line; a recording that now names nothing withdraws its old items. `chintanctl regenerate --instance dev --tenant <id> (--note <id> \| --all)` prints the count and a price-table estimate (the worker's own per-call reservation), `--apply` after a typed `yes` (`--yes`) queues one task per note with no ids and the worker selects for itself; house convention (dry run default, `--apply`) rather than a `--dry-run` flag. Frontend: ⋮ → "Regenerate from recordings…" → plain confirm ("Re-does the AI cleanup of N recordings with the current settings; edits you made inside those paragraphs are replaced. ### S2 · Routing

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| R6-RT-1 | Name-first filing: the ring says the note's name and runs into content ("App feedback checklist move seems to be good", "Business ideas by Priyanka seated pool for dogs"); the model titled both as new notes on 2026-09-27 and the owner moved them by hand. It runs for a `new` decision and, one step past the brief, for an append under the 0.75 bar, so a name-first capture files whichever unsure verdict the model gave. The log line carries `matched_by` (title\|alias\|tag\|prefix_title\|prefix_transcript); `RouterTitleMatchedExistingNote` counts it, no new dimension. `routing.NormalizeSpeech` is the comparison form (also what `MentionsInstruction` used inline). Spans that do not fit are left for `RemoveSpans` to refuse; the content is still the transcript with words deleted. `ponytail:` "note" is the one trailing noun the battery showed; "list" joins it the day a row shows it. Rows 1, 8, 15, 16 (confidence under 0.75 once each) get no code guard: a "the transcript contains the note's name" rule would have filed row 8's topic mention into Portugal trip, and row 1's dip came from three "Roof repair" titles on the test tenant; they stay the fixtures' to measure (PR B). |

### S1 · Checklist intelligence

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| R6-CL-1 | Checklist items as a one-level tree: shared prompt rules, Split up guards, merge into existing parents. Prod battery before: 10 of 15, every failure a group ("eggs from Walmart and meat from Costco" → `Eggs from Walmart`, `Meat from Costco`; "for the party plates, cups and napkins" → three flat; the ring's "Shopping list eggs from Walmart" → `Eggs from Walmart`); the owner's live Split up of 2026-09-29 gave "Add milk to…", "Add eggs to…", "Add protein powder…" | **done** (backend; S1) | `cleanup.Item{Text, Done, Children}`; one rule block `checklistItemRules` (what an item is; app words out incl. a spoken title prefix; split X and Y; group as the person grouped — a place, person, occasion or category is the parent, one level, never invented; remove/tick as spoken; garbling and fillers; nothing lost) composed by the items prompt (six examples, the owner's two sentences first) and the Split up prompt (the list as it stands → the list it was meant to be; keep every line's meaning; keep and join groups; duplicates merge, open wins; done stays done; the owner's case beside an existing Costco as the worked example). `ParseItems` reads objects or bare strings, clamps a grandchild, lifts an empty parent's children, caps at 100 counting children; `RenderItems`/`ItemsFromLines` are the clean artefact's format (a child two spaces in, no box; the body's box read as done). `TasksPrompt(body, title, language)` names the list first; `NotePrompt` refuses tasks; `NoteOutput(raw)` is the document check alone; `SplitOutput(raw, body)` renders `- [ ] `/`- [x] ` lines with the indent, drops an item whose words are not the body's (a dropped parent's children lifted), and refuses a lost tick, a reopened one (unless the body had an open duplicate) and an invented one; the verbatim-done-lines-in-order rule is gone. Caps: items 4× / floor 768, tasks 3× / floor 512. `LLM.CleanNote` gains `title`; `ChecklistItems.Items` and the fake's `ItemsResponse` are `[]cleanup.Item`, the fake's tasks default answers the body's lines as JSON. Pipeline: `extractItems` stores `RenderItems`; `checklistItems` keeps a two-space indent; `keepTick` reads and writes ticks at any depth; `mergeChecklistItems` (the lens's merge oracle, twelve cases) joins a parent to its existing top-level line, drops an open duplicate, reopens a done one and its parent, never crosses a marker, never adds a tick, counted as `ChecklistItemsMerged{Outcome}`; a first append merges then puts the rest under the marker (a bare trailer marker when nothing is left); a takeover of an interrupted first append and `paragraphInNote` re-run the merge over the body without the paragraph, which adds nothing twice. **Decision taken here:** `replaceChecklistItems` always goes by words for a checklist (it used to replace the paragraph whole when the marker still held it), because a merged child can stand inside another recording's paragraph and Regenerate would have dropped it; and a fourth Split up guard, the invented tick, was added beside the brief's three, since a tick the model adds hides an open item under Done. Known limit, documented in `checklists.md`: a merged child lives in the parent's paragraph, so deleting the recording that owns that paragraph takes the child with it, and deleting the child's own recording leaves it. Fixtures: 17 items cases and 8 tasks cases (`fixtures.json`, `want` as indented lines; `top_level`, `count_in`, `excludes_any`, tasks `list_title`); `prod-battery.md` gains the checklist battery with the tree rows and the merge row. Docs: `checklists.md` (append rule, the merge and its limit, the `tasks` mode, the indent paragraph), `prompts.md` (items and tasks), OpenAPI `NoteCleaned.mode` description. **Owner gate before merge:** `LIVE_LLM=1 LLM_API_KEY=… go test ./internal/provider -run 'TestLiveEval/(items\|tasks)' -count=3`, then the checklist battery three times at 15/15 (`prod-battery.md`). Deliberately not built: depth two; applying "remove milk"/"tick off eggs" to the list (still returned as spoken); parent-name synonyms in the merge (`ponytail:` ceiling in `append.go`). |
| R6-CL-1 review | Review of #155: one must-fix (a recording that joined an existing parent took that parent out — and left its other children dangling — when it was retranscribed, regenerated with other words or re-extracted as `no_content`, since `replaceChecklistItems` treated every previous line as the recording's; confirmed as `- [ ] Chicken\n  - [ ] Meat` and `  - [ ] Meat` alone), eight nits | **done** (backend; S1) | `replaceChecklistItems` leaves a taken top-level line standing when a sub-item it is not taking is still under it, and puts the fresh items in through `mergeChecklistItems` over the list without the taken lines — the recording's parent joins the kept line, the rest goes where the first taken line stood — with ticks carried into the merge (a done child written done, no parent reopen for it; a done leaf never merges); the same-words Regenerate now keeps the block's order and is pinned as the unchanged body, plus five table cases run twice each. Nits: `ChecklistItemsMerged` counted after the write returns, not inside the retry closure; one `llm.FoldWords` (case, punctuation, whitespace) replaces cleanup's and the pipeline's two `foldWords`, so `passport.` and `Passport` are one item to the merge as to Split up; an item with no letter or digit is dropped in `itemText` (and `checklistItems`), so `—` is neither stored nor a tick nothing can account for; two Split up guards — a childless open answer item that is part of a done line and of no open line is a reopened tick (a parent is exempt: a group's name over done lines), a done answer item with an open body line's words and no open answer item closed the open one of a pair; `Item.done` read leniently (`"yes"`, `1`); the takeover re-run's known reopen of a tick placed inside the lease window is a sentence in `appendToNote`; `regenerate.md`'s "replaced there" corrected. Flagged to S4 (R6-CL-2), not touched here: Split up's `ChecklistPreview` (`ChecklistEditor.tsx`) sets no `data-depth`, so a nested proposal previews flat while adopting writes the indented body the Items tab renders nested — the real editor S4 puts in its place closes it. |
| R6-CL-1 review · correction | The row above says `Item.done` is read leniently as `"yes"` or `1`. The code reads a bool `true` and the strings `"true"`/`"yes"` as done and anything else — a JSON number `1` included — as open (`cleanup.Item.UnmarshalJSON`, `backend/internal/cleanup/items.go`; `items_test.go` "a mistyped done is read, not refused" pins `1` → open) | **done** (docs; double-blind DB6-41) | Against a `- [x]` body line a `1` is therefore a lost tick and `SplitOutput` refuses the whole answer, the safe side; against an open line it is an open item. The code is unchanged; this row corrects the claim. |

### Flakes · the append claim race

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| R6-FL-1 | Flaky test: `TestConcurrentCompleteCaptureAppendsExactlyOnce` demanded that two concurrent completions of one capture both return nil, while the claim section of `append.go` makes the duplicate lose by design. Reproduced on orb with `go test -race -run TestConcurrentCompleteCaptureAppendsExactlyOnce -count=200 ./internal/pipeline/`: 3 of 200 runs failed, every one `pipeline: complete capture append: repository: version conflict` — the loser had found the paragraph already written, gone into `finishAppend` beside the holder and lost `CompleteCaptureAppend`'s conditional write; `errAppendClaimHeld`, the loss the code comments describe, did not fire once in that loop | **done** (backend) | The test encodes the design instead of the lucky ordering: at most one delivery errs, and only with `errAppendClaimHeld` or `repository.ErrVersionConflict` from the completion race; the text is in the note exactly once; the capture is `appended` with `AppendedAt` set by the time both have returned; a third run (Lambda's retry) returns nil, leaves the text once and the note's stamp cleared. The `AppendStampPoll` override and its comment went: with one capture id `anotherAppendInFlight` is false, so this test never waited on a stamp; `TestAnAppendWaitsForAnotherCapturesFreshStampAndNotForAStaleOne` covers that path. New test under the same loop: 200 pass, the loser seen in 2 runs, one of each error. **Not done, for the owner:** `CompleteCaptureAppend` is idempotent in sequence (`AppendedAt > 0` returns the row) but not under concurrency — on a lost conditional write it returns `ErrVersionConflict` even when the same token was just completed by the other attempt, so a duplicate that arrives after the holder's body write fails its invocation for nothing, logs `capture pipeline could not complete` and counts a `CaptureStageFailures` before the retry finds the capture terminal. A re-read on `ConditionalCheckFailed` that returns the row when `AppendToken == token && AppendedAt > 0` would make the completion what `append`'s and `finishAppend`'s comments already say it is; the test's tolerance of `ErrVersionConflict` goes with it. |
| R6-FL-2 | `CompleteCaptureAppend` was idempotent for its token in sequence but not under concurrency: a duplicate delivery that found the holder's paragraph already in the note went into `finishAppend` beside the holder, lost the completion's conditional `PutItem` to the holder's completion of the same token, and got a bare `ErrVersionConflict` — the invocation failed for nothing (`capture pipeline could not complete` at ERROR, a `CaptureStageFailures` count, a Lambda retry that found the capture terminal), and the R6-FL-1 test tolerated the conflict as a wart | **done** (backend, #163) | On a lost write the completion reads the row again (`repository/dynamo_captures.go`): the same token completed, by whichever attempt, returns that row with nil; the same token still unfinished — the duplicate's `appending` status write, which carries the token forward, moved the version — retries the write from the fresh read, bounded at three tries (`completeAppendAttempts`); any other token stays `ErrVersionConflict`. The `Store` doc says so. `captureRacingDynamo` takes the concurrent write as a func, and `TestCompleteCaptureAppendIsIdempotentForItsOwnToken` slips each of the two writes in under the completion and asserts the row handed back is the stored one and that a second token still conflicts; on the old code both cases fail with `version conflict`. `TestConcurrentCompleteCaptureAppendsExactlyOnce` admits `errAppendClaimHeld` as a loser's only error; the wart paragraph went. `-race -count=200`: 200 of 200 pass; `-count=1000`: 1000 of 1000; `-cpu 1,2,4 -count=200`: 600 of 600 — no loser observed in any of the three on this run (R6-FL-1 saw two in 200), so the completion race is pinned by the repository test, not the loop. Full gate green (gofmt, vet, `go test -race ./...`, golangci-lint 0 issues). No design doc described the failing duplicate; none changed. |

### Wave 3 · Routing — the double-blind findings and the battery triage

The double-blind review (`docs/reviews/2026-09-29/double-blind-r6.md`) numbers its findings by report; the reconciled findings list this wave worked from numbers them by position, and the ids below are the list's, with the report's in brackets where they differ. The triage is `docs/reviews/2026-09-29/routing-triage.md`.

On an append the title is the destination's name spoken as the instruction's last words, so an instruction-only append still ends empty. Review of the PR: the fallback is bounded to a title longer than a name (`maxNameWords` = 5, the R6-RT-8 Titles sentence's own bound), so a naming-only recording whose span stops before or inside a real name ("Create a note with the title test123", fixture case 4; "title this staging smoke", row 13's span shape) still ends empty as at #151 instead of keeping the name or its tail as body. Test: `TestRouteKeepsTheDictationWhenGrowingOverTheTitleWouldEmptyIt` (the sentence kept, the two naming-only shapes empty, the instruction-only append empty). |

Nothing adds a word; the empty-content guards apply after the growth. Post-deploy: rows 9, 13, 23, 24, 26, 29 three times. |

| R8-F1a | Checklists held one level of sub-items (CL-D1); the owner asked for more (F1) | **done** (backend + `checklist.ts`; stream A of `spec-checklists.md`) | Three levels, `cleanup.MaxDepth` = `MAX_DEPTH` = 2, pinned by `max_depth` in `checklist-lines.json` and asserted by both suites. Both parsers clamp to one under the item before and to the maximum, so a deeper body is clamped, never dropped; a body at depth 0 or 1 reads as before. The merge (`pipeline/append.go`) reads depth through `cleanup.ParseLines`: a reopened leaf reopens every ancestor, a parent's children are matched among its direct sub-items, and a regenerated recording keeps a line that has the person's own line under it at any depth. `SplitOutput` keeps three levels. **DB6-11 settled** as "the parent reopens": a done item never has an open descendant, in Split up, the merge and `toggleItem`. Voice extraction stays one level (owner decision 2, default). `shiftLevel` / `canNest(by)` are ready for the editor (stream B); until B lands, a third-level row has no CSS indent. Tests: `TestMergeLeafReopensEveryAncestor`, `TestMergeParentMatchesDirectChildrenOnly`, `TestParentOfAtDepthTwo`, `TestChecklistItemsKeepsTwoLevelsOfIndent`, `TestReplaceChecklistItemsKeepsAThreeLevelBlock`, `TestSplitOutputKeepsThreeLevels`, `TestSplitOutputReopensADoneParentThatGainsAnOpenChild`, `TestParseLinesClampsAndReadsPastMarkers`, the "three levels" block in `checklist.test.ts`. **Owner**: `TestLiveEval/(items\|tasks) -count=3` with the key (a new three-level tasks case). |
| R8-F1a | Review of #191: a regenerate whose every old line stayed as a shared parent dropped its new words; Split up let an invented group reopen a done line; DB6-9 read as settled | **done** (backend + CSS) | `replaceChecklistItems` counts a released shared parent as found and writes the new lines right after its block (DB6-7's fix): Costco › Meat with a line typed under Meat, regenerated to Costco › Meat, Rice, now writes Rice (`TestReplaceChecklistItemsWritesANewChildWhenEveryOldLineIsShared`). `SplitOutput`'s DB6-11 exemption holds only when the open child's body line names the group, the body had it open under that line, or it is such a group itself; `- [x] Milk`, `- [ ] Eggs` answered Milk › Eggs is refused. `ParseLines` lets a prose line reset the clamp as the editor does (fixture case "prose between items"); the Go fixture test fails on a count mismatch instead of skipping. `[data-depth='2']` indents level-3 rows before the editor stream lands. **DB6-9 stays open** (owner): a blank or a marker still ends a parent's block for the merge, so `Costco › Meat`, blank, `<c_2>` `  Rice` plus a recording of Costco › Rice adds a second Rice; `TestMergeParentBlockStillEndsAtABlankLine` pins it. |
| R8-F1b | The editor for three checklist levels (F1, stream B of `spec-checklists.md`); #191 left a level-3 row dragged one level out previewing at the top level, because the relative `data-nest-preview='-1'` rule set the indent to zero | **done** (frontend) | Tab / Shift+Tab, →/← on the grip and the menu move one level, all read through one planner (`plan` in `ChecklistEditor.tsx`) over `shiftLevel` / `canNest(…, by)`. A sideways grip drag is 24 px (`--space-6`, read live) per level, unclamped in `useDragReorder` (`levels` is a number now) and cut down by the editor to the largest move that fits ([−d, maxIn − d] and the row's own sub-items), so one release can move several levels; the preview is the absolute `data-preview-depth`, on the lifted row and every sub-item it carries, with one CSS rule per level that outranks `data-depth` (the bug fix). The first open row is never indentable and a row goes at most one under the row above; refusals are said from the grip with fixed sentences (new: "Already three levels deep", "Its sub-items are already three levels deep"); moves are said once, where they ended ("Made a sub-item of “Costco”", "Moved up a level, under “Party”", "Now a top-level item"). A parent carries its block. Rows, open and done, have `aria-level`; the field "Sub-item N, level 3" at depth 2. No narrow-phone step (the spec's width budget). Tests: `ChecklistEditor.test.tsx` "three levels" (10 cases), `checklist.spec.ts` (Tab to level 3 across a reload; a touch drag two levels in one release across a reload, then one level out previewing 24 px; a vertical drag moves a three-level block intact; opt-in depth and preview screenshots in both themes). |
