## Round 9 — 2026-10-01

| Id | Finding | Status | What changed |
|---|---|---|---|
| R9-BE1 | Tidy accepted a model answer that dropped open items and prose lines (`Milk / Eggs / Bread / Rice` answered as `Milk` was stored as `- [ ] Milk`, err nil); since #195 the answer is written over the body, and the design doc said the guards refused it | **done** (backend, r9/loss-fixes) | `cleanup.SplitOutput` has a coverage guard: every open body line, prose lines with words included, must be in the kept answer by words — an item that is the line, a part of it (one line split into several) or that holds it (several merged into one); otherwise the whole answer is refused with the fixed `an open item was lost` (`ErrNotATaskList`), which the worker records as the `nothing usable` verdict and the client shows as "Couldn’t tidy the list." Only an open answer item covers a line (a done one with its words is the line closed). The tick guards are unchanged. For the owner: the guard is the safe default and refuses some honest answers, so "Couldn’t tidy the list." can now appear where it did not — most likely on a prose→checklist conversion, whose lines were never items: a heading line the model folds into a group it names differently, a filler-only line ("okay so", "um"), sign-off prose ("that's all for now"), a merge of two lines that share no words, and a garble the model fixes by changing the word ("flour" for "flower") all read as a dropped line. Try again is the recourse; the list is never shortened. Table test: the repro, a merge of two lines, a split into three, a regroup under a new group, a prose line dropped, a prose line kept. `checklists.md` lists the guard. |
| R9-BE2 | A move never stamped the target note, so an editor save on the target between the paragraph's insert and the row refresh passed the ETag and version checks and stored a body without the paragraph, which the source cut then removed from its only other home | **done** (backend, r9/loss-fixes) | `moveInto` stamps the target with `StampNoteAppend` before the insert and clears it in the step-4 refresh (`ClearAppendStampFor`), the mechanism the worker's append uses, so `UpdateNote` answers `ErrAppendInProgress` and the client repeats the save once the move has landed; every other exit, the R7-1/R7-1a undo paths included, hands the stamp back with `ClearNoteAppend`. A target another recording is being appended to right now refuses the move (`ErrMoveIncomplete`) rather than overwriting the worker's stamp. Tests: a save during the move is refused, the paragraph ends in exactly one note, the stamp is gone afterwards and the repeated save lands; a failed cut leaves no stamp. |
| PR9-8 | Seven word-splitting rules coexisted: `ask.Tokenize` kept the emoji variation selector `llm.IsWordRune` drops (the pre-R7-2a rule), the recording filename slug kept enclosing marks, `provider.normalizePhrase` (added in R7-10d, after R7-2) dropped every combining mark, and `match.tokens` split on `[^a-z]+`, so a Malayalam or Hindi note had no tokens in `MatchNotes` | **done** (backend, r9/be-fixes) | One splitter: `llm.Words` (exported `comparableWords`). `ask.Tokenize`, `match.tokens` and `filenameSlug` call it. `match` changes behaviour, from ASCII letters only to digits and every script: "Q3 plan" now tokenises `q3` as well as `plan`, and an Indic title ranks by token overlap instead of exact substring only; `normalizePhrase` is gone and the silence check compares by `llm.FoldWords`. `routing.NormalizeSpeech` already did. `service/search.go` keeps its whitespace split on purpose: it is a substring search, where a word rule would stop "amara.org" matching. `TestEveryWordSplitterAgrees` (in `service`, the one package that reaches every splitter) feeds Latin, Malayalam, Hindi and emoji strings through all of them and asserts one word list; match is checked through `Rank` on a title that differs by punctuation only. For the owner (D7): the chillu fold (`ask.FoldScript`) is still applied in Ask and search only, not in `llm.Words`, since folding it in the merge changes which stored lines compare equal. |
| PR9-26 | `SetCaptureTarget` with `new_note_title` made a fresh-id note before the row write: a crash or 5xx between the two (which the idempotency layer does not replay) left an empty orphan note per attempt | **done** (backend, r9/be-fixes) | The new-title path goes through `CreateNoteOnce` with the capture's own note id (`service.RoutedNoteID`, moved from the pipeline so both the router and this path name the same note); the repeat finds the first attempt's note. | PR9-11 | FE-7: a queued edit the server refused (409 on replay) was marked dead with its text unreachable; `queue.ts:258` promised a reconcile UI that did not exist | **done** (frontend, r9/fe-fixes) | The conflict banner, not a Discard: once online, `useNoteEditor` reads the dead entry's PATCH back (`queuedEditBody`), fetches the server's copy, and commits the ordinary `conflict` with the queued fields as my side (`draft` on the event, `draftFields`), so Keep my edits re-sends them on the newer version and Use the newer version takes theirs and forgets the entry (`clearQueuedEdit`, the banner's count included). When the server has not in fact moved — the attempts ran out on something transient — there is nothing to ask and my side is re-sent at once, one shot: the entry is forgotten and the dirty draft owns the text, so an edit refused on its merits (400, 413, 422) shows its error with Try again rather than being re-sent on every visit. A landed save forgets the queue's entry (and refreshes the offline banner's count) only when something was held. Smaller than a Discard would have been honest: a Discard loses the text the offline promise said was safe. Tests: `offline.spec.ts` (409 on the flush → "This note changed elsewhere" with my text in the body, Keep my edits lands it; Use the newer version shows theirs and leaves the queue empty). |

| PR12-20 | F2: a recording of background noise still filed a note. Measured first, from the production worker's `transcribed capture` lines (read-only, metadata only). Scores have been on the line since 2026-09-30, so 45 captures carry them (42 dated 10-01, 3 dated 10-02; the 192 earlier lines have none, and the 8 `ml` captures all predate scoring — `segments.json` stores no scores). All 45 carried `no_speech_prob_min` 0 — Groq's `whisper-large-v3-turbo` never reports it — so the rule-13 pair (`no_speech_prob` > 0.6 and `avg_logprob` ≤ −1) could never fire. Of the 45: 30 were the QA battery's identical English clips (identical logprobs); 11 real English dictations, `avg_logprob_max` −0.08…−0.604; 1 stock silence phrase at −0.8806 (11.6 s, one word; the phrase list caught it, the scores would have kept it); 3 noise filings — `c_18da4df1…` (2.2 s, 1 word, "Portuguese", −2.08, routed `new`, title_words 1), `c_18da6b10…` (5.4 s, 2 words, −2.56, routed `new`) and `c_18daba12…` (1.6 s, 13 words, −1.35, routed `new`, title_words 13). Clips: three noise clips and a text-to-speech control were generated on orb with ffmpeg (`~/temp/noise/`: pink −28.2 dB mean/−14.4 peak, room tone −46.4/−35.1, fan −24.5/−12.0, control −15.6/−2.0; 6–7 s, webm/opus 32 kbps) and `provider.TestLiveNoiseProbe` prints each segment's text and three scores — but orb's default AWS profile is a `login_session` that has expired (`aws sts` answers "session has expired, run aws login"), and the agent profile is denied SSM, so the clips were not sent. Owner: `aws login` on orb, then `K=$(aws ssm get-parameter --name /chintan/dev/groq_api_key --with-decryption --query Parameter.Value --output text --region us-west-2); cd ~/temp/chintan-r12pipeline/backend && LIVE_STT=1 GROQ_API_KEY=$K STT_PROBE_DIR=~/temp/noise go test ./internal/provider -run TestLiveNoiseProbe -v -count=1`; paste the table into routing.md's gates section and this ledger. |
| PR12-21 | F2: the client signal and the row. The recorder already measures the loudest frame; a recording whose peak never crossed the recorder's own floor is "nothing heard" | **done** (backend + frontend, r12/pipeline) | `PeakCollector.max()` → `RecorderController.peak()` → `peak` on `POST /v1/captures` (0..1 to three places, absent when nothing measured; `StoredCapture.peak` so a resume sends it too) → `CaptureIndex.Peak`. `pipeline.transcribe` files a capture with `0 < peak < routing.QuietPeakRMS` (0.04 = `PEAK_FLOOR`, under which the live canvas never rose) as `no_content`, gate `quiet`, before any provider call, under the same `transcribed capture` line (`gate=quiet`, `peak`). The gate that ended a capture is on the row and the wire (`gate`: quiet / no_speech / hint_echo, null for the instruction-only no_content; promoted beside `excerpt` and in the by-note hydrate projection). Filing tray and Recordings tab say "Nothing heard" for a gated row, "Nothing to save…" otherwise; a refused recording is never dropped without a row. Transcribe again (`RetranscribeCapture`) sets `skip_gates` when the row carried a gate: the next run sends the audio whatever its peak and files the words whatever their scores, refusing only the prompt read back and a transcript with no letter in it; a "Nothing heard" tray row has Transcribe anyway beside Dismiss (`useTranscribeAnyway`, the same `/retranscribe`; announced "Transcribing again." / "Could not transcribe that again."), so a routed capture has the override too. `peak` is sent only when the recorder collected frames, so 0 means heard nothing and absent means unmeasured (`*float64` on the server). Tests: `TestAQuietRecordingIsRefusedWithoutATranscriptionCall` (zero provider calls, then the override appends), `TestTranscriptOutcomeOrdersTheThreeGates` (the override cases), the service retranscribe table (the gate lifted only when there was one), `peaks.test.ts` `max()`, `uploader.test.ts` (peak absent / 0 / 0.05), `model.test.ts` and `labels.test.ts` ("Nothing heard"), `FilingItem.test.tsx` and `e2e/capture.spec.ts` (Transcribe anyway reaches `/retranscribe`). OpenAPI: `CaptureCreate.peak`, `Capture.gate` with a `captureNothingHeard` fixture holding a non-null gate on the wire; fixtures regenerated the CI way. What the numbers justified: the capture screen was a lazy chunk, one round trip after the main chunk on every cold shortcut launch; it is in the main chunk now, and the Details/Share drawer (`NoteDrawer`, 10.5 kB) and `zipRecordings` with `fflate` (9.3 kB) left it as lazy chunks (`shell.md` "What a launch downloads"). Already in place or inapplicable, so not touched: the preconnect to the API and Cognito origins (in the live index); a JWKS preload (the client never fetches JWKS — the verifier is the backend's); the notes-list and settings deferral on `/capture` (`fetchList` holds both until `recording`; `apiBefore 2` is the commit that paints "Recording"); mic-first after auth (the screen's first effect, now without the chunk gap). The CPU's share on orb is small (warm launches ~150 ms); a phone's is larger, but what was removed is a network round trip. |
| PR14-21 | B12: recording while the screen locks | **done** (frontend, r14/launch) | The device truth from the Web APIs is the table in `capture-ux.md` "What happens when the screen locks". Nothing stops on the hide itself: Android keeps `MediaRecorder` running behind a locked screen, and a stop there would truncate a take that survives. Shipped: on `visibilitychange` hidden the recorder calls `requestData()` so the chunk it is holding (≤ 3 s of speech) is on disk, the machine marks `hidden`, and the capture record is rewritten with the real duration on the hide and on every chunk after it (it carried 0:00 until review, so a page the OS killed mid-take was offered back by `ResumePrompt` as 0:00); a `mute`, `ended`, recorder error or spontaneous `onstop` behind a hidden page is marked `background`, and the screen says "Paused — the screen locked or the app went to the background" (Cancel · Resume · Stop · Send; `unmute` resumes by itself) or "The recording stopped while the screen was locked or the app was in the background. What was captured before that is here." (Discard · Re-record · Send). Two machine fixes found on the way: a final chunk nobody asked for (`onstop` with no Stop) is settled as an interruption instead of leaving "Recording" over a dead recorder; a track that ends before the first chunk now waits for the recorder's final chunk instead of failing at once with "Recording was interrupted." (a call in the first three seconds lost those seconds). Tests: `machine.test.ts` "the screen locks", `recorder.test.ts` "the page goes hidden", `store.test.ts` "behind a locked screen", `e2e/capture.spec.ts` "the screen locks while recording" (hidden, then `mute` or `ended` raised on the real stream; the sentences, the controls and the IndexedDB record's duration). |

Android expected still Recording with both sentences in the note; iPhone expected either the pause-and-resume pair or Home's unsent-recording prompt at 0:10. Any other outcome is the report; the row stays open until both phones are run. |

| PR15-10 | Bounded retry with full jitter inside both provider clients on 429/5xx/529 and transport faults, honouring `Retry-After`, under the stage's own deadline | **done** (backend, r15/retry) | `provider.retrying` (`provider/retry.go`) wraps `OpenAICleanup.complete` and `GroqSTT.Transcribe` (URL sources only; a `Body` gets one attempt). Bounds in `provider/bounds.go` with a registration pin (`TestProviderBoundsAreRegistered`): `ProviderRetryAttempts` 3, `ProviderRetryAttemptsAsk` 2, `ProviderRetryBaseWait` 1 s, `ProviderRetryMaxWait` 4 s (waits under 2 s then under 4 s, six at most). The budget is the context's: a wait that would not end before the deadline is not taken and the last refusal is returned at once; a cancellation during a wait returns promptly with both errors. `StatusError.RetryAfter` carries the header; one past `ProviderRetryMaxRetryAfter` (12 s) is returned at once. The budget guard covers the wait plus a request as long as the refused one, so a retry the deadline would cut — a timeout to the stage, Lambda-retryable — is not started (review of this PR, M1). The transport class is narrow (`*net.OpError`, ECONNRESET, a body cut short, a connection closed before the response line); an unresolvable name or an unverifiable certificate is not retried. `ProviderRetried{Provider,Status=429\|5xx\|transport}` with a rollup, listed in `metrics.md`; `ProviderRateLimited`/`ProviderTimedOut` unchanged because the stage sees the last refusal itself, wrapped with the attempt count. Thirteen `httptest` cases in `provider/retry_test.go` (529-529-200; 529×4 fails with the last status after 3 requests; `Retry-After` seconds and date; a long `Retry-After`; deadline shorter than the wait, and than the wait plus the request → one request; 4xx other than 429, DNS and TLS faults → one request; cancel mid-wait; connection reset; Ask twice; URL vs Body). Premise correction: a 529 at a capture stage was never retried by Lambda — `handleProviderError` classifies a 5xx as a verdict (`failed`, nil to Lambda); only a deadline is retryable. |
